# Open Questions & Decisions Log

Decisions and the questions that shape the plan. An answered question moves to **Decisions** with
date and rationale. Agents: read this before changing scope. Entries stay terse: decision + why +
where codified. The KB doc is the canonical home of each rule; this table is the dated index, not a
second copy.

## Decisions (answered)

| Date | Decision | Rationale |

| 2026-06-16 | **Commit-log conventions:** a single `[doc]` prefix for pure documentation/process commits; code & build commits stay bare verb-first; docs describing a code change fold into that commit; grouping decided by a revert/cherry-pick cohesion test | Scannable `git log`, cohesive commits → CLAUDE.md, handbook §7 |
| 2026-06-16 | **Gradle migration scope & shape:** Maven→Gradle (wrapper 9.5.1) + Java 17→25 (toolchain) + Micronaut 3.8.4→4.10.3 incl. devcontainer and `Dockerfile`, landed as **one cohesive commit** (the phases weren't standalone, failing the cohesion test); **Micronaut 5 jump deferred** until 1:1 is re-proven (backlog H9) | Up-to-date tech but 1:1 preserved; one framework risk at a time |
| 2026-06-19 | **Shared Eclipse formatter:** one central `config/eclipse/formatter.xml` (tabs, `lineSplit=180`) consumed by VS Code, Eclipse, IntelliJ (adapter) *and* Spotless; format + organize-imports on save; **star imports forbidden** (later expanded to fully wildcard-free); only two deviations carried (chain-wrap one-per-`.`, no trailing method-body blank); `.editorconfig` considered and **dropped**; full reflow accepted: uniform enforced standard over hand-tuned wrapping | One standard across IDEs + build from one file; JDT is the only engine all consumers share → build-run-deploy.md |
| 2026-06-19 | **Braces → Checkstyle `NeedBraces`, single-purpose:** the first choice (VS Code `addBraces` cleanup) proved **non-existent** in redhat.java and was scrapped; Checkstyle covers only the brace gap the formatter cannot fill and is **not** a general linter (that earlier rejection stands) | Real enforcement + IDE support; one rule per tool, no overlapping systems → build-run-deploy.md |
| 2026-06-20 | **Acceptance test as a synthetic, committed fixture** (not a PII prod snapshot): `TestDataGenerator` writes `testdata.sqlite`; real server + real evaluator, stubbed scraper; assertions through the JSON envelope. **Code must be self-explanatory without external documents** (a backlog-ID test name was rejected) | Synthetic data is committable and reproducible with zero PII risk → testing.md |
| 2026-06-20 | **Branching, merge & review gateway:** two tracks, chosen up-front by the planner: *small* → directly on `main`, one approved commit; *large* → feature branch/worktree, agents self-commit each TDD step, falsifier + reviewer review the diff, **author + planner review the branch log as the final gateway**. Merge = **rebase + `--ff-only`**, no squash. Every `main` commit is **verified-good** (a red build, a fake-green commit and a reviewer-rejected commit all count as broken); a gateway defect is repaired **by rebase into the owning commit**, never a follow-up fix commit; rewording via scripted (non-interactive) rebase | Keeps the TDD cadence without N approval round-trips; the author keeps a single veto point and a linear all-green history → handbook §7, multi-agent-playbook.md |
| 2026-06-20 | **No logic in constructors; field assignment only.** Construct+initialize orchestration belongs in a static factory (`SqliteDatabase.open(...)`) or a lifecycle hook | Logic in constructors breaks testability and SRP → handbook §3 |
| 2026-06-20 | **Avoid `static`; treat it as a smell**, above all mutable static state; prefer DI / instance state. Accepted exception: a one-time **test-code** initializer that must run before the framework context boots | Crystallised from a mutable static stomping across two boot-test contexts → handbook §3 |
| 2026-06-20 | **Per-class JVM test forking (`setForkEvery(1)`) introduced, then removed the same day:** once table creation moved into the repo factory and the mutable static delay became per-context config, boot tests share one JVM safely (also removed ~240s test waste) | The fork was a crutch for nondeterministic startup, not a fix; superseded by deterministic production ordering |
| 2026-06-21 | **`Category.storage` removed (dead field):** never read; the recipe-based valuation already yields identical values, pinned by an all-items golden master. The hunt-loot estimate stays a separate open question (D-4, gates E3) | Vestige of a superseded valuation model; zero value change, verified 1:1 |
| 2026-06-22 | **Durable docs must stand on their own; no dead backlog-shortcode references:** identify things by symbol/behavior/file/concept; a shortcode only as secondary pointer to a **still-live** item; completing an item removes its row and every referencing shortcode | Completed items leave the backlog, so bare shortcodes go dangling and defeat "docs are knowledge" |
| 2026-06-22 | **Prefer immutable `record`s; no getter/setter boilerplate; `Logger` last in constructors** | Less boilerplate, immutability by default, scannable signatures → handbook §3 |
| 2026-06-26 | **Tests must be deterministic; never wall-clock-dependent:** wait on a real signal (no-timeout latch, released on success *and* failure paths); a timeout/retry is a mitigation, not a fix | Must pass on any hardware; crystallised replacing a boot-signal poll timeout with a latch → handbook §6 |
| 2026-06-26 | **Code/build is the single source of truth; docs must not duplicate volatile facts** (versions, dependency lists, counts); prose points at the code | Restated facts rot silently and had already diverged → handbook §3 |
| 2026-06-26 | **Commit `gradle/gradle-daemon-jvm.properties` deliberately:** pins the daemon JVM portably, no host-specific or secret data | Reproducible and machine-independent for any checkout |
| 2026-06-28 | **Permission config unified for the worktree flow:** one portable committed wildcard `Bash(git -C *.claude/worktrees/*)` guarded by `-C` deny variants (`push`, `reset --hard`, `clean`); genuinely portable rules promoted into committed `settings.json`; session-/host-pinned cruft emptied from `settings.local.json` | The "always allow" flow kept writing dead host-pinned rules, one per worktree, re-prompting every time → handbook §7, working-with-ai-agents.md |
| 2026-07-03 | **Falsifier panel, gate escalation & session-model doctrine:** the falsifier splits into two cheap-tier lenses (`falsifier-domain`, `falsifier-robustness`); gates escalate per feature via a one-off `model` override to the strongest tier the plan offers (Fable when available, else Opus; **amended 2026-10-12**: no lens runs on `fable`); the main-session model is picked per session job (strongest for planning/review, smaller for routine TDD) | Two cheap lenses cost about one mid-tier run and add diversity; session limits mean spend goes where it converts (planner + gates) → multi-agent-playbook.md, working-with-ai-agents.md |
| 2026-07-03 | **Setup-review triage (author calls):** the bug track (malformed date, watermark, WebDriver leak) goes before the Gildenmehrwert metric; the bundled webdrivers and the local-browser machinery are removed, then the author rewrites history himself; `Erde-Eibenlanze` is the real spelling (the code has the typo); python stays in the devcontainer (fix the nested features object); Dependabot adds `docker` + `gradle`; hook activation outside the container becomes a documented one-liner; the Claude home stays a bind mount (existing defenses judged sufficient); the falsifier panel stays read-only (scratch counter-tests via Bash); the record-equality tests are deleted; the ArchUnit inward-only rule extends to the core; a KB citation guard is added; the service runs without a reverse proxy; schema migrations get a real framework (the game serves only 30 days of logs, the DB is the only history); `git-state.md` becomes `git-conventions.md` | Full setup review 2026-07-03, findings triaged as multiple-choice with the author; fixes run through the normal pipeline → backlog D11, F1, G18, H11 + the build hardening (landed 2026-08-01) |
| 2026-07-04 | **Vulnerability scan: OWASP dependency-check → Trivy (binary, not Docker):** `./gradlew vulnScan` builds a CycloneDX SBOM (`org.cyclonedx.bom` plugin) and scans it with the `trivy` binary installed by a devcontainer feature; the NVD feed, API keys and the scan-secrets machinery (incl. `secrets.local.properties.template`) are gone; report-only by default, gated via `vulnScan.failOnSeverity`. Closes the prebuilt-NVD-DB-image evaluation: the Docker-image variants (owasp/dependency-check, trivy image) lost to the static binary because the devcontainer has no Docker | The NVD datafeed download was slow and kept aborting (author 2026-07-04); Trivy's DB is small, keyless, pulled from an OCI registry and cached under `~/.cache/trivy` → build-run-deploy.md |
| 2026-07-04 | **Frontend stack: React 19 + TypeScript + Vite** (Vitest/RTL, ESLint `typescript-eslint` strict + `eslint-plugin-import-x` boundary rules, Prettier), resolving **D-6** in favor of a JSON API + SPA over more HTML-string templates; built as three worktree strands (`frontend-build` → `json-api` → `spa-views`) | Small single-guild dashboard: React's ecosystem and Vite's fast dev/build loop fit the TDD cadence better than the previously-undecided Angular option → frontend.md |
| 2026-07-04 | **The SPA recreates Evergore's visual style itself; no game assets (images/CSS/markup) are copied into the repo** | Keeps the repo a clean-room showcase and avoids redistributing the game's assets |
| 2026-07-04 | **The current endpoint paths stay** (`/overview`, `/avatars/{avatar}/bank`, `/avatars/{avatar}/storage`) **and become the SPA's client-side routes** rather than being renamed | Preserves existing bookmarks/links; the SPA shell already serves at `/` and falls back to it for unknown navigation paths (strand `frontend-build`) |
| 2026-07-04 | **Token-filter scope inverts to `/api/**` only, deferred to strand `json-api`**; once inverted, `/swagger/**` etc. become public since they no longer sit behind the same gate | The SPA shell and static assets must load without a token; only the JSON API needs auth; keeps strand `frontend-build` scoped to build wiring only. **Superseded 2026-08-05** by the default-deny decision below: inverting to `/api/**` only would have left every unmapped path and both legacy HTML pages public |
| 2026-07-06 | **The frontend's npm dependency scan uses `trivy fs --scanners vuln --skip-dirs node_modules`, not `npm audit`** (re-planned, replacing an earlier `npm-audit`-task plan) | One scanner (Trivy) covers both the JVM (CycloneDX SBOM) and npm dependency graphs under a single `vulnScan.failOnSeverity` gate, avoiding a second tool/output format → build-run-deploy.md, frontend.md |
| 2026-07-06 | **Skills review (`/pause`, `/continue`, `/optimizeSettings`):** slash commands migrated from `.claude/commands/` to `.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md`; `/pause` runs fully autonomously: it signals running subagents to finish only their current micro-step (never kills, never waits for full task completion) and parks uncommitted work as ONE local `[wip]` commit (`--no-verify`, the one sanctioned bypass) that `/continue` must resolve first through the confirmed-message gateway; `/continue` slimmed to KB pointers (no rule copies) and starts without a go round-trip when the backlog "▶ next action" is unambiguous; `/optimizeSettings` additionally scans recent session transcripts under `~/.claude/projects/` | Duplicated rule copies drift (KB-first); an unambiguous next action was approved when written down; compaction hides earlier approvals; at pause time the author may have no time for input, so the commit gate moves to /continue while `[wip]` keeps the tip visibly non-deliverable → handbook §7, `.claude/skills/` |
| 2026-07-11 | **Permission tiering (autonomy-first, ground rules hold):** grow the agent's autonomy by **generalizing** observed allows into portable rules and **never blindly reverting** them (even ones a parallel session added); but destructive/irreversible/exfil-capable ops are **never a blind `allow`**. This pass promoted `java`/`javac`/`stat`/`git cherry-pick`/`nohup ./gradlew`/`xargs stat`/`xargs echo`/single-URL-localhost `curl`/`Read(/usr/lib/**)` → allow; `git checkout`/`git -C * checkout`/`dd`/broad `xargs`/`curl` → ask; `rm:*` → deny (the agent never runs `rm`, it hands over the command). A localhost `curl` allow cannot bound curl's multi-URL exfil, so single-URL-localhost-only is an **agent behaviour rule** (proper fix later: a PreToolUse hook, backlog G7/G13) | The "always allow" flow kept repolluting the committed `settings.json` with un-portable, path-bearing rules + tabs→spaces; codifying the tiering **and the autonomy-first / don't-revert principle in the `optimizeSettings` skill** stops future runs from reverting it → `.claude/skills/optimizeSettings/SKILL.md`, `process-learnings.md` |
| 2026-07-17 | **Roadmap & risk register as living docs:** `docs/roadmap.md` (milestone order + milestone-level acceptance; items and their acceptance stay in the backlog) and `docs/risks.md` (risks + countermeasures); completed milestones and handled risks are removed like backlog rows | Sequencing and risk knowledge had no single home; separate files keep the backlog item-scoped (DOC-4/DOC-5) → roadmap.md, risks.md |
| 2026-07-17 | **Data integrity before the dashboard:** the watermark rework plus the catalog fix and the domain confirmations precede the dashboard strands | Until fixed, every aborted run permanently under-counts and the 30-day log window makes the loss irreversible; the dashboard would render wrong numbers |
| 2026-07-17 | **Falsifier panel: third lens + per-surface composition:** `falsifier-frontend` joins the panel; a gate spawns only the lenses matching the touched surface (backend → domain+robustness, frontend-only → frontend+robustness, full-stack → all three); pure `[doc]` changes gate on the `doc-reviewer` alone (the `reviewer` only on genuine process doubt) | Gate effort should track what the change can break: a backend diff gains nothing from a frontend lens, a doc diff nothing from falsifiers; spend stays where it converts → multi-agent-playbook.md, `.claude/agents/falsifier-frontend.md` |
| 2026-07-17 | **Gate escalation extends to auth/security-surface changes** (filters, token handling, what is publicly exposed) | The `/api/**` token-filter inversion is an auth change yet matched no escalation trigger; a security regression is as costly as a valuation one → multi-agent-playbook.md |
| 2026-07-17 | **DB backup stays a monthly manual routine (author); no automation item** | The cadence suffices for a hobby: after a restore the 30-day log window re-scrapes most of the gap; revisit if the cadence slips → risks.md |
| 2026-07-17 | **Parser domain confirmations: `Einzahlung` stays mapped to `EINLAGERUNG`; the `+1` modifier is value-neutral and keeps merging with its base item** | `Einzahlung` occurs in real protocols but warrants no distinct behavior (author); a `+1` item is value-equal to its base, so merging loses nothing; both get pinned by contract tests and documented in domain-model.md |
| 2026-07-17 | **Meta sums: full recompute per run replaces the planned watermark rework** (every evaluation recomputes the per-avatar sums from all stored entries; `last_updated` becomes display-only, written after a successful run; **its meaning and write condition superseded 2026-09-03**, see below) | Ingest dedup never used the watermark and the DB keeps every ingested entry, so recomputing is idempotent, removes the DST/skew/same-minute/partial-write defect class at the root and heals historical drift (incl. zero-valued catalog misses) automatically; per-run cost is negligible at guild scale → domain-model.md |
| 2026-07-17 | **Ingest dedup covers the whole scraped window, not just its newest minute:** the extractor ingests the surplus of the scraped rows over `repo.getAllSince(min scraped timestamp)` (occurrence-counting, so two identical same-minute entries stay legitimate); this heals a still-visible protocol entry that never reached the database (e.g. an earlier, buggy strict-cutoff scrape), however far in the past it sits relative to the stored max, not just at the exact boundary minute. Extended to the whole window 2026-07-19 (author) | Without it the "no known silent-loss path" claim would be false, and the loss is permanent (30-day log window); a boundary-only fix still misses a still-visible row that is older than the stored max → architecture.md |
| 2026-07-18 | **Bank withdrawals are worded `Entnahme` in the protocol; `Auszahlung` does not occur there** (it is only the Google Sheet's column label); an unmatched timestamped protocol headline now warns loudly instead of silently dropping | A silent drop systematically understates sums; the falsifier proved the drop path → glossary.md, `EntryFactory`/`EntityParser` |
| 2026-07-19 | **Timestamps move to an epoch/UTC storage format, gated on the migration framework; no interim code change** | The falsifier panel proved the default-TZ `DATE_STRING` storage breaks round-trip equality and the lexicographic max in a DST fall-back hour, and the window dedup makes value equality load-bearing; fix the format at the root instead of pinning the container TZ (author); until then the service runs under a fixed DST-free TZ (container default UTC) |
| 2026-08-01 | **The devcontainer gets `firefox-esr` as the interim browser; H2's remote `selenium/standalone-firefox` stays the planned end state** | Makes the scrape path locally runnable *today* with no code change (`Browser.DOCKER` builds a local headless `FirefoxDriver`, Selenium Manager fetches the geckodriver). H2's `RemoteWebDriver` rebuild is a separate, testable change; it then drops `firefox-esr` from the image again. Alternatives offered: do H2 now instead, or leave the path untestable |
| 2026-08-01 | **Per-class test forking (`forkEvery`) is removed for good and guarded in the build** (measured in the build-performance analysis; confirms the 2026-06-20 decision, which a later feature commit had silently reverted). The suite runs in one JVM: `cleanTest test` **1m01s** instead of ~9 min, green in 5 consecutive runs, 1 fork instead of 198. Alternatives offered: parallel forks, or a split boot-test suite, both rejected as workarounds for a problem that does not exist | All three suspected causes were disproven: `BootSignalRecorder` has no static state, `Configuration.useInMemory`/`DATABASE_TEMP_SQLITE` are unwritten *instance* fields, and each `@MicronautTest` already owned its own SQLite file. A worktree at the very commit that (re-)introduced the fork is green without it. Gradle restarts the JVM per compiled test **class file** (198, only 28 with tests) and `--tests` filters inside the worker, so a single focused test cost 19m33s, so real TDD was impossible → testing.md, `build.gradle.kts` |
| 2026-08-01 | **SonarLint is declared as a VS Code extension, not as the third-party `sonarlint` devcontainer feature** | That feature is a no-op (its `install.sh` only `echo`s; its whole effect is two extensions plus a `dependsOn` on `node`), so it added supply-chain surface for nothing. Its old GPG breakage came from the `node` feature's yarn apt repo, which node 2.x no longer uses. The real static-analysis gate remains backlog **G6**. Alternatives offered: re-add the feature, or keep deferring |
| 2026-08-01 | **TypeScript stays on 6.x; the TS 7 bump waits for typescript-eslint support.** The frontend npm refresh raises every declared range to the version it locks, so Dependabot's in-range PRs (eslint, prettier, vite, typescript-eslint) are superseded rather than merged; only the TS 7 PR stays open. Alternatives offered: run TS 7 side-by-side with the `@typescript/typescript6` alias feeding the linter, or take TS 7 and drop typescript-eslint from the lint config | Reproduced against the real config: `tsc -b` passes under 7.0.2, but `eslint .` exits 2 with `typescript-eslint does not support TS 7.0` (peer range `>=4.8.4 <6.1.0`), so `:frontend:npmLint` and the whole build go red. The cause is upstream and not fixable here: TS 7.0 ships no stable programmatic API (it returns in 7.1) and typescript-eslint closed the support request as not planned. TS 7 buys compile speed, and `tsc -b` costs about a second on this SPA, so a dual-compiler setup would be pure overhead → `frontend/package.json` |
| 2026-09-03 | **The timestamps' interim safeguard is a startup validator, not pinning the process zone to UTC** (**superseded 2026-10-17**: the validator is retired, see the row on the startup rule "the service starts in any time zone") (backlog item "Reject a DST-observing effective zone at startup"; revises the 2026-07-19 "no interim code change" decision above, scoped to the ledger `DATE_STRING` columns only): it rejects any effective zone whose rules are not a fixed offset and aborts the boot naming the zone, pattern `CredentialsStartupValidator` | Failing loudly surfaces an operator's zone misconfiguration instead of silently overriding it; pinning the process zone would mask that same mistake with no error, and is discarded for that reason. The zone is injected like the credentials are, so the reject path is a plain unit test and never touches the JVM's actual default zone. Scope/limits: the superseding row of 2026-10-17 below |
| 2026-08-04 | **JSON API wire shape:** resource-oriented and versioned (`/api/v1/avatars`, `/api/v1/avatars/{avatar}/bank\|storage`); English field names spelled correctly (`withdrawn`, `deposited`); every collection answers the same envelope (`page`, `size`, `totalCount`, `items`), the overview additionally carrying `lastUpdated`; instants as ISO-8601 UTC and `transferType` as `DEPOSIT`/`WITHDRAWAL`, both localized by the client. Alternatives offered: mirroring the HTML routes, German sheet terms, keeping the `withdrawl` typo, pre-formatted timestamps, paging deferred to `spa-views` | The typo must not reach a public contract, so renaming it internally stays a non-breaking change; `totalCount` lets the SPA size its navigation instead of guessing from a short page; a version prefix makes the client renameable later → frontend.md |
| 2026-08-04 | **No wire or column name is ever derived from a Java identifier.** Every wire record component carries an explicit `@JsonProperty`, every `@DatabaseField` its `columnName`, every `@DatabaseTable` its `tableName`, and `RenameSafetyTest` fails the build on a missing one. `transferType` goes out as a `String` filled by `TransferTypeWireNames`, a visitor over the domain enum, so the German domain constants stay renameable too. Alternatives offered: a Jackson mixin per record (records stay import-free, but a rename is then only caught by the acceptance test, and Micronaut 4.10 ships no `@JacksonMixin`, so it needs a hand-wired `BeanCreatedEventListener`), or an explicit mapper to a `LinkedHashMap` (strongest guard and zero foreign imports, but the responses lose their OpenAPI schema) | A rename must never be a breaking change, and an implicit field-name mapping makes every rename one. There is **no** JDK-standard annotation for this: JSON binding never landed in Java SE, and `jakarta.json.bind`'s `@JsonbProperty` is not read by Jackson, so it would pin nothing. The coupling can only be placed, not avoided, and it sits where the name it pins sits, in the adapter layer that ArchUnit already allows framework imports in → frontend.md |
| 2026-08-04 | **The token scope is default-deny, not an enumerated deny list** (supersedes the criterion "invert the filter to `/api/**`" of milestone M2, the JSON API, done and since removed from roadmap.md): every path needs a token except the ones listed under `evergore.security.public-paths`, matched by `PublicPaths` against the canonicalized path. The list lives in `application.yml` next to the static-resource mappings it mirrors. Alternatives offered: a `@Public` annotation on the exempt controllers plus a code list for the static mappings, or the same list as a Java constant | An enumerated protect-list cannot be maintained by a human: a new controller would be public until somebody remembers it, which is a silent hole rather than a visible failure. An annotation covers only two of the nine exemptions, because `/`, `/assets/**` and the OpenAPI UIs are static-resource mappings and `/health` is Micronaut's own endpoint, so there is no class of ours to annotate. Config binding puts the list in one place, changeable without a recompile → build-run-deploy.md |
| 2026-08-04 | **Public surface accepted:** `/`, `/index.html`, `/assets/**`, `/favicon.ico`, `/health` and the OpenAPI UIs (`/swagger/**`, `/swagger-ui/**`, `/redoc/**`, `/rapidoc/**`) need no token. Only `/`, `/index.html` and `/assets/**` were additionally free of the rate limit and the request log (**superseded 2026-08-14**: nothing is exempt any more), the rest are counted and logged like any request. **A tokenless deep link therefore answers 401**: the shell loads from `/` and the client carries `?token=` across its routes | The SPA shell must load before it can send a token, and its assets carry no guild data: the bundle is the same clean-room code that is in the repo. Rate limiting *those* would throttle a normal page load and logging them would bury the real request log in asset noise, which is not true of the remaining public paths. Everything that carries guild data sits behind the token, including every unmapped path → build-run-deploy.md |
| 2026-08-04 | **JSON serialization is pinned in `application.yml`, not left to the framework default:** `jackson.serialization-inclusion: ALWAYS` and `write-dates-as-timestamps: false` | Both defaults silently broke the agreed contract: instants went out as epoch decimals, and the non-`ALWAYS` inclusion dropped empty `items` arrays and would have dropped the explicit `lastUpdated: null` that means "no run yet" |
| 2026-08-04 | **Javadoc is an absolute no-go** and counts as a comment, not as documentation; the same goes for explanatory `//` lines. Non-obvious knowledge goes into a name, a **test name**, the KB or this file. `reviewer` and `implementer` now flag every doc comment a diff adds, not only "unnecessary" ones | The handbook's "no comments unless intent can't live in names/structure" already covered it, but the `json-api` strand grew Javadoc on eleven files and both `reviewer` and `doc-reviewer` passed it, so the rule needed to name Javadoc explicitly to be enforceable → engineering-handbook.md |
| 2026-08-04 | **Tests rank above production code, and every test is strictly arrange/act/assert** with the act as its own named value; an act buried inside the assertion is a review finding of its own. A custom assert helper is the alternative shape and holds the same structure inside. `junit-jupiter-params` is declared so one behaviour over many inputs is a `@ParameterizedTest` instead of a repeated assertion | The tests are the specification the author reads, so care spent there outranks care spent on the implementation; a one-line `assertThat(call().field())` hides both the act and what is actually being exercised → testing.md |
| 2026-08-04 | **Every persisted time is an `Instant`; the UI applies the zone** (author decision, first recorded in the backlog item on timestamps as instants) → domain-model.md, `last_updated`; architecture.md, "Last activity comes from the ledgers" | `last_updated` was a `LocalDateTime` all the way into `MetaInformationKey.DateTimeKey`, so it never saw a zone, and the JSON API had to reconstruct an instant from a Berlin wall-clock value, which is ambiguous inside the hour the clocks go back |

Alternatives offered: keep the literal HTML parity (404 for an empty ledger) and record the ambiguity, or answer 204 for the empty case | The falsifier proved it on the committed fixture: `Calix` has bank rows but no storage rows, so `Calix/storage` and `Nobody/storage` were the same 404 and a client could not tell "this member never stored anything" from "this member does not exist" without a second request. That contradicted the strand's own promise that every collection answers the same envelope, and it would have been a breaking change to correct after `/api/v1` was published. The legacy HTML pages keep the old 404 and disappear with `spa-views` → frontend.md. Accepted cost: on the empty path the controller counts both ledgers, so one of the two counts is taken twice; threading a second supplier through `pageOf` to save it would cost a sixth parameter on a method the reviewer already called too wide, and the query is one indexed count on a guild-sized table |

The deletion rule gains one narrow scope: inside that gitignored directory an agent may delete files it created in the same session (**that carve-out is superseded 2026-08-06** by the `clearProbes` decision below; the rule keeps no exception). Everything tracked by git, and everything the author wrote, still gets handed over as an `rm` command. Alternatives offered: no repo footprint at all (probes in the session scratchpad against the compiled classes, which does not fit `@MicronautTest` server-boot probes), or a name-based exclusion filter inside the existing test source set | The rule protects the author's ownership of **his** code, and a subagent's throwaway probe is not that. What actually forced the round-trip was mechanical: a probe under `src/test/java` turns `./gradlew build` red and `git add -A` commits it, so the author's `rm` sat on the critical path of every gate round (twice in this strand). Moving the probes out of git's and the build's sight removes the *need* to delete rather than the *rule* against it; a leftover becomes harmless hygiene. The name-filter variant was rejected because a mis-named real test would then be silently skipped, which is a fake-green risk exactly where it hurts most → backlog G11 |

Alternatives offered: a constant reason phrase, or a JSON error body; for the guard: a parameterized test over today's four visitor methods, both, or no guard | The requested avatar reached the reason phrase, so a percent-encoded `CRLF` in an unknown avatar's name made Netty refuse the response: a caller-triggerable **500 with an `error`-level stack trace** instead of a 404 with one `info` line (reproduced end-to-end before the fix). A constant phrase only restates Netty's default and keeps a second call site of the overload alive; a JSON body would contradict the 404/401 empty-body contract the client codes against. The value stays visible in the log through the request path, so nothing is lost. | 2026-08-14 | **The per-IP counter map is bounded by an idle sweep plus a hard cap** (`evergore.rate-limit.max-tracked-clients`, `10000`): when a new client appears, clients whose interval elapsed and that are not blocked are forgotten (their counter would reset anyway, so no decision changes), and if the map is still full the least recently seen client goes. The bound wins over a running block, and any value that would silently disable the throttle (a request or client budget below `1`, a non-positive interval or block duration) is refused at startup. **No legitimate client is ever refused for capacity** | Alternatives offered: cap plus LRU only (keeps dead entries until pressure builds), idle sweep only (no hard bound against many distinct IPs inside one interval), or fail closed at the cap (a spurious 429 for a legitimate client, and a cheap DoS lever). The clarification behind it stands and is carried into C11: for an *authenticated* user the interesting audit event is which overview or avatar he opens, not the resources his session then fetches, and the rate limit is aimed at scanner bots | The only carrier that reaches a browser-issued asset request is a cookie, which would give the service session semantics and a second credential type it deliberately does not have (HMAC-signed and IP-bound it needs no store, but it needs scope rules: a nonce that can be minted anonymously over `/` hands a scanner an unmetered surface for one request). The gain is small: the tokenless shell bootstrap over `/` fires four anonymous requests itself, so the anonymous budget must carry a page load either way, and content-hashed assets are re-fetched only on a cold visit. A token bucket (burst plus a low refill rate) buys the same "a page load is one spike" semantics while every request stays counted, and it belongs in the JWT rework rather than in front of it → backlog C11 |

Alternatives offered: one filter named after both jobs, or `RateLimitFilter` keeping the log line inside. The docs claimed a fixed name that never existed on the target, and the placeholder variant would have cost the property the fixed name exists for: every deploy and rollback command copy-pasteable under time pressure. Alternatives offered: move the bump to the tip by reverting and re-setting it (two ceremonial commits today, and every later release would have to end on a bump commit), or tag the version commit as written | The version commit `30d4214` sits 20 commits behind the tip and is the commit whose **image build is broken** (fixed in `bfe0b28`); it also predates the credential injection, the global rate limit, the avatar union and the Selenium determinism work. Tagging it would point the release at a stand that was never built or run, while the tested image came from a different commit → build-run-deploy.md |
| 2026-08-16 | **The Evergore login is injected as two environment variables, not as a mounted file, and the `FileLoader` port stays out of it.** Chosen over a mounted `zugang.txt` and over a Docker secret: the mount buys only that the value stays out of `docker inspect`, while adding a second silent-mount failure mode next to the database one; a Docker secret means Swarm or Compose, which would rewrite the freshly rehearsed `docker run` deploy. `FileLoader` resolves every path against `evergoreFolder`, so it could not have loaded a mounted `/zugang.txt` unchanged anyway; it stays the geckodriver loader it is. Missing or blank values fail at **startup** (`CredentialsStartupValidator`), exactly like the API token | The image becomes secret-free, the deploy grows by two `-e` and nothing else, and a forgotten login is a dead container instead of a healthy one scraping logged out. Accepted cost: the values are visible in `docker inspect` and in the deploy shell's history, the same exposure the API token already carries on a single-admin home server → build-run-deploy.md |
| 2026-09-02 | **The corrected storage sums go live with the overview's storage columns and the change is announced in the guild; no correction booking** (closes the 2026-08-09 detail question): the recompute *raises* the stored sums by roughly 40 % (`storage_placement` `77,723,347` → `108,542,193`, `storage_withdrawl` `226,534,092` → `310,087,852`, all 42 avatars upwards) and six avatars stand at exactly `0.0` in both storage keys despite years of stored entries (Ivory, Malak Almawet, Maesch, Mightypanda, Elazia Kapp, Thyla Vom Moos, all last active 2023/2024) while their bank meta matches the SQL sum exactly, so the two key families were filled by different mechanisms and the storage one never covered the full history. The drifting mechanism itself stays unidentified, and the recompute heals it either way. Alternatives offered: decide when the feature lands, or investigate the drifting mechanism first as its own item | Same line as the `0.1.0` bank drop: the recompute is the correct number, so a correction entry would only freeze the wrong one into the data for every future recompute. Nothing served moves until the columns exist, which is why the announcement rides with the feature instead of standing alone (superseded 2026-10-10: the guild hears nothing before 1.0) |
| 2026-09-02 | **The overview grows to the sheet's columns 1-5 plus a guild-wide total row:** bank in/out and storage in/out as four separate columns, the net per avatar, and a total row over all avatars. Alternatives offered: per-ledger nets only (narrower table, but deposits and withdrawals stop being separable), or the four columns without a total row | The four sums are what a member reconciles his own row against, and a net alone cannot be checked against the sheet; the storage half is already computed and stored every run, so serving it is a wire change, not a domain one → frontend.md |
| 2026-09-02 | **The time filter is one parameter pair, `?from=`/`?to=`, and `from` may be relative** (`-24h`, `-7d`, with `to=now`), resolved server-side; presets are **mixed rolling and calendar-aligned** (24 hours and 7 days rolling, this week from Monday 00:00, this month from the 1st, all time the default), and the arbitrary date range ships in the same cut, so the former date-range item is **absorbed** rather than left beside it. Alternatives offered: a `?range=24h` shorthand next to `?from=&to=` (two mutually exclusive forms, 400 when both are given), or presets resolving to a concrete `from`/`to` in the address | A preset bookmark must keep its meaning: resolving "last 24 hours" into an absolute window at click time freezes the bookmark on that window, which defeats the reason the filter state is in the URL. One parameter pair keeps the contract narrow, at the price of a duration parser with its own 400 rules. No window can come from the meta store, which holds all-time sums only → backlog E12 |
| 2026-09-02 | **"Gildenmitglied des Monats" awards all four titles and shows the last completed month beside the running one:** Materialsammler (deposited item count), Jäger (the same over `JAGDBEUTEN`), Goldgeber (bank deposits), Wertschöpfer (net over the month). Alternatives offered: the last completed month alone (a title that stands for a month), or the running month alone | The two item-count awards need no valuation rule, so they sidestep **D-4** entirely and honour the gathering the value model rates at zero; the standing title plus the running standing costs one more aggregation over the same query → backlog E13 |
| 2026-09-02 | **The overview truth cut goes next** (the four ledger sums, the net per avatar, the guild-wide total row and German collation as one track), ahead of the ledger paging defect, the time filter and the easter egg; **B20 no longer leads the queue**. Alternatives offered: paging first (the one outright defect of the six user requests), the windowed aggregation first (most leverage, no visible result until a UI sits on it), or B20 first as the roadmap had it | The overview is the officer-visible payoff and the headline metric the sheet exists for, and three of the six new requests land in it; B20 blocks nothing and M4 (ingest and test robustness, since dissolved, B20 moving to roadmap.md's ongoing test-suite hygiene) is otherwise done → roadmap.md |
| 2026-09-02 | **The net per avatar is derived, never stored:** a framework-free `AvatarContribution` over the four ledger sums answers `net()`, and the read side maps it onto the wire; no fifth meta key. Alternatives offered: a stored `guild_value_<avatar>` written per run (the item's "compute & store" read literally), or deriving it in the SPA from the four sums | A persisted derivative can drift from its four summands, and a fifth key family would join the schema migration and D9's rename scope for what is one subtraction; the same record carries the windowed sums that E12 and the Wertschöpfer award need in Java anyway → domain-model.md |
| 2026-09-02 | **The guild-wide totals are computed server-side and ride in the overview envelope** next to `lastUpdated`, over every known avatar rather than over the served page. | 2026-09-02 | **The last bank and storage activity is queried from the ledgers, not stored as meta keys:** one query per ledger yields the latest timestamp per avatar. | 2026-09-02 | **The overview stays a flat eight-column table whose two activity columns carry the ledger links**, so the link-only `Lager` column goes and `Column`'s `timestamp` kind gains an optional href. Alternatives offered: seven columns with one merged activity column, or a grouped two-row table head mirroring the sheet's merged headers | Every column head then sorts something real, which retires the standing finding that the `Lager` head offers a sort it can never perform; it is also the smaller change to the table primitive, a second head row with column spans being the larger one → frontend.md |
| 2026-09-02 | **The overview's storage sums and net reach the wire as whole gold** (`long`), each sum rounded **before** the net and the guild total are computed from it, rather than as the value model's raw doubles. Alternatives offered: `BigDecimal` at two decimals (the catalog's precision as a stated contract, always in plain notation), or keeping the raw doubles and documenting them | Jackson renders a `double` through `Double.toString`, so every value from 10^7 upward, which is where the real per-avatar sums sit, would reach a client in exponential notation carrying float noise; the small fixture numbers that make the acceptance golden look clean are an accident of the test data. The sheet shows whole gold and the SPA already rounds to no decimals, so whole gold is what a member reconciles against, and rounding before summing keeps every row internally consistent and the total row exactly the column sum of the rows above it → frontend.md |
| 2026-09-03 | **The meta store stays the overview's producer; the torn read is closed at the port, not by aggregating the ledgers live**: the recompute hands its whole result to `MetaInformationRepository.add` as one batch written in one transaction, and the read side answers a response from one `MetaInformationSnapshot`; the per-key `get` is gone from the port. **Live aggregation was measured, not estimated,** on the production snapshot (42 avatars, 245,072 rows): a meta-store read costs ~120 ms, a live aggregation 24.4–25.6 s as the code stands and 1.23–1.61 s at best case (single scan, hashed catalog), growing linearly with the log forever | Keeps the **1:1-verified** quantity where the scripted 1:1 value comparison reads it, keeps a correctness fix independent of **E12**'s performance design, and needs no answer to the repository unification (the meta port has one implementation, unlike the two ledger repositories). E12 still owns the windowed aggregation; all-time simply is not served from it |
| 2026-09-03 | **A recompute isolates failures per avatar; the failing avatar keeps its stored sums and is named in `/health`** (falsifier finding, same day): the batched write would otherwise have turned one unreadable ledger row into a permanent guild-wide freeze, because `getAllFor(avatar)` still dereferences `timeStamp` unconditionally and throws before the batch is written | Narrows a full-run freeze back to per-avatar staleness without giving up the single transaction the torn-read fix relies on, and follows the existing unknown-item reporting path rather than inventing a second one |
| 2026-09-03 | **`last_updated` means "when data was last scraped from the game", and is stamped on every completed run, including one in which an avatar failed to recompute** (author clarification): it is an operator's datum, not a per-row freshness claim | Scrape time and recompute completeness are separate facts, and tying the timestamp to the second would make `/health`'s `lastSuccessfulRun` and the overview's `lastUpdated` contradict each other, and could leave `lastUpdated` null beside real sums on a guild that never had a fully clean run. "How current is this member's row" is answered by that row's own two last-activity columns instead. Moving the timestamp off the overview is a separate change |
| 2026-09-03 | **The gate for sequencer-created commits is a recorded breach, not a refusal:** `post-rewrite` (amend, rebase) and `post-commit` (`cherry-pick`, `revert`, told apart by the reflog) scan the commit plus the resulting tree, write the offending shas to `.git/gate-breach`, and `pre-commit` refuses every further commit while one is still reachable from `HEAD`, clearing the record by itself once none is. `git am` and a merge commit are the exceptions that are refused outright rather than recorded, because `pre-applypatch` and `pre-merge-commit` run before their commit exists. A plain or `--no-verify` `git commit` is deliberately left alone. Alternatives offered in the analysis: a `reference-transaction` hook (the only hook that can genuinely *refuse* a rebase, but it fires on every ref update including `fetch` and `stash`, so the format leg would run in contexts that cannot afford it), or leaving the hooks to print a loud warning | git ignores those hooks' exit status and has already moved the refs when they run, so refusing is not on offer and printing is exactly the narration the finding condemns. Recording turns the bypass into a hard stop one commit later, and reachability as the clearing condition keeps the reset-free fold usable: rebuilding the branch without the offending commit ends the block with no manual step. Recording a `--no-verify` commit too would turn the documented emergency valve into a trap → build-run-deploy.md |
| 2026-09-03 | **`pre-commit` refuses every added `//` or `/*` line in `.java`/`.ts`/`.tsx`, with no exception and no opt-out.** A scanner over the file's post-image marks the lines carrying a comment outside every literal (quotes, backtick templates, `"""` text blocks) and those lines are intersected with the lines the diff adds, so a URL or an XPath in any of them is not a comment; only **added** lines count, so the 22 existing comment lines in five files stand until a commit edits them; `.kts`, Markdown and every other type stay out of scope; and there is no hook-side switch, `--no-verify` or otherwise, that turns just this check off. Alternatives offered: a raw line match with an `http(s)` carve-out, a licence-header exception, whole-file checking, and an `EPC_ALLOW_COMMENTS` escape | A check that produces false positives gets switched off, so the recognition was measured before it was chosen: over the whole tree it marks lines in exactly the five files that carry comments and in no other, and a line-wise variant was rejected because it reads a TypeScript template literal and a Java text block holding an XPath as comments. A TypeScript regular-expression literal containing `//` is the known residue. Whole-file checking would block the next commit to `EvergoreDataExtractor` for a comment that commit did not write, and a licence-header carve-out would be stock for a header the repo does not have. It closes the 2026-09-02 slip where ten comments survived two falsifier rounds and three doc-reviewer rounds, because none of those lenses reads code comments → engineering-handbook.md, build-run-deploy.md |
| 2026-09-04 | **Backlog order re-cut, and a parked set named.** Order: correctness first (proving the recompute has run in production, at `P0`, taking in the scripted 1:1 value comparison, the scrape/recompute split, the timestamp move and the per-row staleness marker), then the overview's actual position (the named levy, the active/dormant roster split), then the two bottlenecks, the repository unification and the migration framework, *before* the product build-out, then what they release (the ledger index, the pre-grouped recompute (**D18**), timestamps in UTC retiring the backlog item "Reject a DST-observing effective zone at startup", the withdrawl rename (**D9**)), then **E12**→**E13**. **Parked as not-in-this-stage**, kept with their rationale rather than deleted: **A4**, **G6**, **F4**, the public read-only OpenAPI JSON API for guild tooling, the short "case study" of the rebuild for the showcase, the `/commit` slash command encoding the strict one-line/no-footer/never-push protocol, and pointing the agent at official docs (WebFetch) for less-trafficked libraries. The G epic is cut to what prevents an error mechanically (**G7**, **G13**, the wildcard-import ban, **G11**). Alternatives offered: product before the bottlenecks, or the repository unification alone with the migration framework deferred; the measured performance work deferred or halved; a dedicated showcase wave instead of gap-filling. **Addendum 2026-09-04 (same day, after the measurement):** **the production-snapshot check does not absorb the scripted 1:1 value comparison.** It recomputes a database from *its own rows* and the recompute is idempotent, so the code is its own reference: the check is a self-consistency measurement and structurally cannot be an independent counter-calculation: code and store carrying the same error leave its diff empty. The scripted comparison is exactly that independent check and is therefore a separate item, moved out of **M6** (numbers you can trust, landed and since removed from roadmap.md) into the ongoing test-suite hygiene list. The order is otherwise unchanged | The repository unification and the migration framework gate five items between them, and **E12** is an `L` item that would otherwise duplicate the same read method a fifth time and run it over unindexed tables, and the ledger index was measured taking the ledger detail query from ~15 ms to ~0.1 ms, and the recompute at ~25 s against ~3.4 s, on 245k rows, with two of the three affected queries in the **request path**. Parking is what turns the remainder back into a work list: 58 open items, of which seven waited on a condition that is not arriving (no CI because the deploy is local, no second guild, no second API consumer) → roadmap.md |
| 2026-09-04 | **The production-snapshot check becomes opt-in instead of `@Disabled`, and the measurement it exists for is recorded.** `@EnabledIfSystemProperty(named = "prodSnapshot.check")` plus a `prodSnapshot.file` path property, both forwarded from `build.gradle.kts`, replace the annotation nobody could leave in place; the stored-versus-recomputed comparison over `metaInformation` is committed as `MetaSumComparison` rather than performed ad hoc. **Measured:** the two pre-`0.1.0` snapshots (16.06., 15.08.2026) reproduce none of their 42 avatars, the post-deploy one (03.09.2026) reproduces all 42 exactly; the pre-deploy drift is a frozen absolute amount, and the eight avatars stored at `0` are, without exception, those whose last storage row is in 2023 | Removing the annotation to take a measurement was an uncommittable edit on the critical path of every measurement, which is why the numbers were never reproducible; and a comparison that lives in the check can be re-applied to the next database instead of rebuilt. The result answers, for the snapshot already on disk, whether production ever ran a full recompute, but says **nothing** about whether the computed number is right, because a shared error between code and store leaves the diff empty. That is the scripted 1:1 value comparison's job → testing.md |
| 2026-09-04 | **The recompute runs after every scrape outcome, success or failure; the torn-ledger risk that follows is pre-existing and stays out of this round.** A scrape aborting mid-write (bank table updated, storage not yet) followed by a recompute on that mixed state is already self-healing, existing behaviour, not something the scrape/recompute split introduces, and is not fixed here: it is tracked as its own follow-up, **D24**, which makes the ledger ingest transactional across both repositories | Splitting "could not scrape" from "could not recompute" needs the recompute to run unconditionally; fixing the ingest's atomicity is a separate, larger change that touches the ledger repositories and is better done once, gated on the repository unification, than folded into this round |
| 2026-09-04 | **D11's swallowed `InterruptedException` is fixed only at `EvergoreDataCollectorJob.initialDelay()`**, the one call site this round touches; the rest of D11 (`ExceptionWrapper`, adapter exceptions, `Slf4jLogger`, dead code) stays open in the backlog | A narrowly-scoped fix keeps this round's diff reviewable; D11's remaining scope is unrelated to the recompute split and does not need to land with it |
| 2026-09-04 | **Two FAIL-loop decisions on the scrape/recompute split:** (a) `EvergoreDataCollectorJob.scrape()`'s broad `catch (RuntimeException)` around `dataExtractor.loadData()` stays as-is, filed as an additional confirmed instance under **D11** rather than fixed here; (b) `/health`'s overall status now tracks the *latest* recompute attempt, via `LastRunStatus.recomputeHealthy()`, going `DOWN` on a failed latest attempt even after an earlier success, rather than staying `UP` forever after the first success | Matches D11's existing scope rather than growing this round's diff; a stale `UP` after a later failure would hide the exact condition `/health` exists to surface |
| 2026-09-04 | **`lastUpdated` leaves the `/api/v1/avatars` envelope ersatzlos and moves to the new `/api/v1/admin/status`** (author decision 2026-09-03, landed 2026-09-06): the overview no longer carries any guild-wide timestamp, and the admin endpoint reports it beside the four scrape/recompute outcome instants and the two name lists `LastRunStatus` holds | It is an operator's datum (scrape time), not a per-row freshness claim, so it does not belong beside numbers a member reconciles per row. Keeping it on the token-protected overview also meant every member request answered an operational fact nobody but the operator needs; the admin surface is deliberately anonymous, at the same trust level as `/health`, since it carries nothing sensitive → architecture.md, build-run-deploy.md, frontend.md |
| 2026-09-06 | **Every database column is `NOT NULL`, and the read paths carry no null branches for what the schema forbids** (author rule, generalized into engineering-handbook §3): the ledger and meta tables are rebuilt with a constraint on every column, and the null tolerance that existed for them is deleted rather than kept as a safety net. Whoever wants a nullable column owns the handling of that null everywhere it can surface | Measured, not assumed: ORMLite reads a SQL `NULL` into a primitive `int` as **0** with no exception, so a nullable `amount` would not have crashed, it would have silently changed a member's sums. A `NULL` timestamp was the loud half of the same class of defect. Declaring the absence impossible removes both, and removes the code that guarded against them: two `latestTimestampPerAvatar` null filters, the meta store's null-value filter and the three tests that covered them |
| 2026-09-06 | **Flyway owns the schema; `createTableIfNotExists` is gone** (author decision, chosen over Liquibase and over hand-rolled migrations on `PRAGMA user_version`): `V1` creates the pre-Flyway tables `IF NOT EXISTS` and the baseline sits *below* it, so it runs on every database: a no-op where the tables are there, a repair where one is missing. `V2` rebuilds all three with `NOT NULL`. Migrations run in `SqliteDatabase.open(...)`, after the `PreDatabaseConnectionHook` and before the connection opens, **not** in the hook itself | Flyway is the recognizable industry standard for a repository that is also a showcase, and `flyway-core` handles SQLite without an extra module (verified). It sits in `SqliteDatabase.open(...)` because five `@MicronautTest`s replace the hook with a `@MockBean` to place their fixture database, which would have silently skipped the migration; from `SqliteDatabase.open(...)` no caller can reach an unmigrated database. SQLite cannot `ALTER COLUMN`, so every such migration is a table rebuild with a row copy, which is why the migration test asserts the rows survive **row for row**. `migrate()` is `synchronized` on a class-level monitor because the collector job's `@Scheduled` bean graph and the first HTTP request build the repositories on two threads, and two `V2` rebuilds racing on a fresh file end in `SQLITE_BUSY`; the old `createTableIfNotExists` tolerated that race, a table rebuild does not. Baselining *at* `V1` was tried first and rejected: Flyway calls any non-empty schema baselined, so a database missing one of the three tables skipped `V1` and died in `V2` on the table that was never created |
| 2026-09-07 | **The `:memory:` database option is deleted rather than repaired** (follow-up to the Flyway decision): `Configuration.useInMemory` and the `:memory:` branch of `getDatabasePath()` are gone; every repository test runs on a throwaway SQLite file | Flyway migrates over its own connection, and sqlite-jdbc gives each connection to `:memory:` a private database, so the app would have opened a second, unmigrated and empty one. The knob had no caller and no test, so keeping it would have left a switch that silently breaks every read, the same class of defect the strict-schema rule exists to remove. Sharing one connection with Flyway was the alternative, rejected as a change to the connection lifecycle for a capability nothing uses → engineering-handbook §6, testing.md |
| 2026-09-04 | **The levy gets a stat header and a Beitrag↔Saldo switch (variant A of three), not a ninth column and not a split total row**: the overview keeps its column count and density, and gains a header stating treasury, material balance and levy collected, with a switch between contribution and balance; *Gildenmehrwert* is renamed to *Beitrag* / *Saldo* along the way. Alternatives offered: **(B)** a ninth column "Abgabe" between contribution and balance, which widens the table, and **(C)** splitting only the total row into gold and material, the smallest change, which fixes the misleading statement but explains nothing | The total row currently adds measured bank gold to modelled material value and therefore reads `−80.545.764` while the treasury stands at `+119.562.181`; the gap is the guild's own levy (`219.202.477`), which is deliberate policy and should be named rather than left inside a number shaped like a balance. Naming it costs no column, whereas the table is already eight columns wide (shipped with 0.2.0) |
| 2026-09-07 | **`0.2.0` is cut at the stale-row marker, the atomic run-status snapshot, the named levy and the active/dormant roster split, and the deploy script drives its deploy; the unvalued-names item follows the release** (author decision): the two `S` correctness items go first, then the two view items on the same rebuild, then the deploy script, which runs this release instead of being written after it. Alternatives offered: shipping `main` as it stands, adding only the two `S` items, or the roster split without the named levy | The Flyway rebuild and the verified sums are worth getting to production, but the overview still states the opposite of the guild's position, which is what the guild actually reads. **Naming the levy does not wait on the unvalued-names item:** the header changes how the levy is *stated* and derives it from the data, that item changes what the material is *worth*, so its landing later only moves the figures inside the same header, while the reverse order would change every number under a caption that still describes it wrongly. the deploy script before the deploy because the `0.1.0` deploy found four silent defects on the hand-driven path, and a script written afterwards is a script never proven against a release → build-run-deploy.md |
| 2026-09-07 | **Backlog rows that were lost in a doc commit are restored from git rather than rewritten** (`ce0238a` dropped the rows for the levy item and the active/dormant roster split, for two items answered since, and the detail question behind the levy item, while later docs kept pointing at them): both come back with their measured figures, the answered items stay removed and their shortcodes are cleared out of the prose that still carried them | Two live items, one of them the most visible defect in the product, were invisible in the only list that holds work, while the roadmap still named them; and a shortcode pointing at a row that no longer exists is exactly what the removal rule exists to prevent. Restoring from `c6303af` keeps the measurements that were taken at filing time instead of paraphrasing them from memory |
| 2026-09-09 | **A TDD micro-cycle commits once, after refactor, not at red or green** (author decision, tightens handbook §4's "≈"): red and green are local, uncommitted steps inside the cycle; only the refactored, green result becomes a commit | An interim commit at red or green is not independently revertable (it either fails or is mid-refactor); one commit per full cycle keeps every commit atomic and safe to revert on its own → engineering-handbook.md §4 |
| 2026-09-09 | **Five calls on the deploy script** (author decisions, options offered for each): **(a)** the script builds the image itself with the version and revision labels, rather than requiring a tag built elsewhere; **(b)** the four verification requests run over ssh against `localhost:<published port>`, not from the work machine against the host; **(c)** the backup keeps the documented `bak-<yyyymmdd>` name, but the script **refuses an existing file of that name** instead of overwriting it; **(d)** `pre-commit` runs `deploy/self-test` for any commit touching `deploy/`; **(e)** the self-test replaces `docker`, `curl` and `ssh` with stubs and asserts effects, not printed text | **(a)** puts the label contract in the repository instead of leaving it in the gitignored, machine-specific `buildAndRun.bat`, where nothing reviews it. **(b)** is reachable whenever ssh is, so a router or DNS problem cannot masquerade as a failed deploy; it does not prove the forwarded path, which is the accepted cost. **(c)** deviates from the option as picked, and deliberately: `V2` rebuilds all three tables, so a second run on the same day would replace the only pre-migration copy with an already migrated database, and the rule that nothing skips the backup would be trivially defeatable. **(d)** is the mechanism that already protects `hooks/`, and the self-test needs neither Docker nor Gradle, so it costs seconds. **(e)** because a check that only prints a refusal is what the earlier gate-bypass finding was made of; the assertions read the journal of commands that reached the remote, the bytes of a real file and the backup on disk → build-run-deploy.md |
| 2026-09-09 | **The deploy script's proof obligation is discharged by mutation testing, not by a red/green/refactor trail**, and the mutations are named here so a later gate re-runs them instead of trusting a claim: remove `--type container` from an inspection; turn the probe's `: >>` into `: >`; delete the `docker stop` echo comparison; delete the empty-`ps` check; hardcode `-p 80:8080`; skip the loaded-against-built layers-and-labels comparison; drop a label from the fingerprint format; turn the `\|\| fail` of the local or of the host's fingerprint read into `\|\| true`; drop the backup's refuse-if-exists guard; drop the `--backup` and the `--database-file` path guards; delete the remote secrets-file cleanup (the trap and both explicit removals); drop the `curl -K` config cleanup; delete the value-less-flag guard; and swap the call order of the stop and the copy in either path. Each one must turn `deploy/self-test` red | A POSIX-sh ops script has no Gradle harness, so there is no red to write first; `hooks/self-test` is the established precedent for proving an ops script by its effects. A mutation that lives only in a conversation is not a proof anyone can repeat, which is the whole objection the falsifier panel exists to raise → engineering-handbook.md §4, build-run-deploy.md |
| 2026-09-09 | **The deploy script is not run against the home server until `0.2.0` is complete** (author decision): no dry run and no interim deploy beforehand, so the release deploy is the script's first real run against that machine. Alternatives offered: a dry run now, which reads everything and mutates nothing; or a dry run plus a full deploy of an interim `0.2.0-<sha>` build with one rollback, which would have exercised transfer, stop, backup, replacement and the rollback path while nothing was promised | Production stays untouched while the release is unfinished. The cost is deliberate and named: every surprise now surfaces during the release itself, the rollback path will never have run, and the `V2` rebuild meets the real production database for the first time on that day. That is why the release run's acceptance starts with `--dry-run` as its own step rather than as an option, why the script's checks were proven by mutation instead of by a live run, and why the first deploy should be scheduled with time to read a log rather than squeezed → build-run-deploy.md |
| 2026-09-09 | **A row whose sums are older than the last collection states that on the wire with one nullable instant, and the marker sits at the avatar's name rather than in a column** (author decisions, four parts): **(a)** `AvatarSummary.staleSumsFrom` is an `Instant` that is present only when the row is stale, and then names the instant its sums come from, while `GuildTotals.containsStaleSums` states that the guild row contains such a row; **(b)** the recompute instant is stored per avatar as **epoch millis** under `sums_recomputed_at_<avatar>`, and "older than the last collection" is decided server-side against the **maximum** over those keys, so no second run-level key exists and a run that fails for every avatar marks no row; **(c)** the marker is a `!` **before** the name plus a stripe on the row, and its German note stands in the DOM at all times and is only collapsed visually, so hover, keyboard focus and a screen reader reach the same text: "Veraltete Zahlen. Letzte erfolgreiche Aktualisierung vom <Zeitpunkt>." **Zahlen** rather than *Informationen*, *Werte* or *Summen*: *Informationen* also claims the two activity columns beside it, which are live and current; *Werte* collides with `Gildenmehrwert` and the stored value of an item, a separate open question; and *Summen* is wrong for the net, which is a difference. *Zahlen* names the five numeric columns and nothing else; **(d)** (**narrowed by the 2026-10-06 entry below to avatars whose sums are stored**) an avatar that has no recompute key gets one at the end of the run, seeded from the newest instant the per-avatar keys already held **before** that run. **Addendum, same day, after the falsifier probe:** (d) originally seeded from `last_updated` and no longer does. Reconstructing an instant from that wall-clock text resolves the Berlin fall-back hour to the earlier of its two passes, so a seeded avatar came out dated an hour before the run that produced his sums and therefore **stale against an avatar stamped in that same run**: the imprecision (b) exists to avoid had re-entered through the seed and flipped the marker itself rather than a displayed time. Alternatives offered: a boolean flag per row, a per-row instant always present with the run anchor back in the envelope, a ninth column, a stripe with the names in the caption, and leaving (d)'s gap open | Epoch millis rather than a second `DateTimeKey`: `last_updated` persists Berlin wall-clock text, an open defect then, and a second wall-clock key would double it; the comparison is also exact. Present only when stale, because a per-row instant on every row is the collection timestamp repeated once per avatar, the operator's datum the 2026-09-04 move to `/api/v1/admin/status` took off the member surface; that is also why the note cannot date the last collection. The `!` beats a ninth column, which **cannot** meet the acceptance criterion, since `SortableTable`'s total row carries the missing-value dash for every column that is not a number, and it beats a stripe alone, which leaves colour as the only carrier inside the row. Without **(d)** an avatar whose recompute fails in every run reads as current, which is the very defect being fixed; the seed is exact because the previous run's instant already stands in the store as epoch millis, and absence then survives only while no avatar carries an instant at all (both narrowed by the 2026-10-06 entry below: the seed now dates only stored sums, so it is a bound, and an avatar without sums never gets one). The two activity columns are **not** suppressed for a marked row: that would discard a true fact to prevent an inference → frontend.md, domain-model.md |

With no activity anywhere there is no reference, and every row stays in the active table: the dormant table is an exception list, and nothing is moved into it without a measurable reason. | 2026-09-22 | **One pooled connection source per database file, no connection held by two threads at once** (author decision; the alternative offered, filing the torn read as an item of its own, was declined): `SqliteDatabase` opens the file once per context, runs the migrations and hands out connections from a `JdbcPooledConnectionSource`, none to a second thread while one holds it, and a transaction keeps its own connection until it ends, except that a commit SQLite refuses (an outside process holding a read transaction longer than the connection waits) returns that connection to the pool with its transaction still open; the three repositories are built on it, and the context closes it when it stops | Measured: one `MetaInformationDatabaseRepository` shared by the writing and the reading thread, the production topology, read `[1, 2]` during an open recompute, because ORMLite's `JdbcConnectionSource` hands every thread the same JDBC connection; the 2026-09-03 closure of the torn read held only between two connections. | 2026-09-22 | **An explicit `busy_timeout` of 10 s on every connection, not WAL** (author decision; alternatives offered: WAL plus a `busy_timeout`, 30 s, the 3 s default made explicit, a proof by an injected short bound, a `PRAGMA`-only pin): every pooled connection of `SqliteDatabase` carries it through the JDBC URL, the Flyway migration's own connection stays as it is, and the proof holds a real exclusive lock on the wall clock, a deliberate exception to handbook §6. It bounds reads, write-first statements and commits: a commit waits up to 10 s for an outside reader to finish, so the meta `add`, which commits twice through `inTransaction`, can wait about 20 s (measured 20.5 s). A transaction that reads before it writes (the recompute) still fails at once under another writer's lock, because SQLite answers `SQLITE_BUSY` there without waiting, which is unreachable in-process while the scheduled job is the only writer | WAL is a persistent property of the file with `-wal`/`-shm` sidecars, so a plain copy of the file (the snapshot harness, the backup, the deploy) could miss committed data until a checkpoint; the job's batched writes hold the exclusive lock for milliseconds; sqlite-jdbc's implicit 3000 ms bound was never chosen; 10 s lets a read survive an unusually long lock instead of answering 500 → architecture.md, testing.md |

| 2026-09-30 | **Parallel gate builds get a machine-wide build lock in `./verify`, not a private Selenium Manager cache per worktree** (author decision, revising the same day's choice of the private cache after the measurement refuted its premise) | Measured before landing anything: the bundled Selenium Manager (1.0.0-M2, from selenium-java 4.7.2) contains no `SE_CACHE_PATH` and ignores `XDG_CACHE_HOME`; probed standalone, only `HOME` moved its cache out of `~/.cache/selenium`, so the backlog row's `SE_CACHE_PATH` would have moved nothing. The geckodriver race the incident named did not reproduce (two Selenium Manager processes started together on a fresh directory, 3 of 3 exit 0; whether they overlapped was not measured). Two worktrees at once, each `GRADLE_OPTS=-Dorg.gradle.daemon.registry.base=<worktree>/.gradle/daemon-registry ./verify all`, beside two to three foreign lanes: on `main` both got through `:test`, `:check` and `:build` and both failed `:frontend:npmTest` after about 56 min; with a private cache (`HOME` under each worktree's `build/`, not landed) the same, after about 45 min (one-minute load 5.7 to 13.3), 22 of 24 vitest suites reported in one of them, fewer in the other. The same vitest suite passed alone (3 min 38 s) and twice at once (3 min 44 s and 3 min 37 s); vitest 4.1.10 starts a fork worker with a hard-coded 90 s timeout that no option raises. So what fails a parallel build is the frontend stage beside a loaded machine, and the cache changes nothing there; the cause of the missing suites is not established beyond that. A lock excludes the load and a cache race alike, which a private cache cannot; it costs the second build its wait and does not cover a direct `gradlew` or `npm` run. Whether it spans the whole build or only the frontend stage is measured in the lock's strand. Alternatives offered: land the private cache as hardening (a geckodriver download after every `clean`, a failing offline browser run right after one, benefit unproven); run vitest with fewer workers. The interim `flock` rule of process-learnings 2026-09-29 stays until the backlog row closes |
| 2026-09-30 | **A figure rounds half away from zero** (author choice from three rounding modes, recommendation taken): every figure the read surface rounds once from its exact value goes to the nearer whole gold, and a half to the larger magnitude, so -0,5 shows as -1 as 0,5 shows as 1 → domain-model.md | Rounding once from the exact value brings negative figures to the rounding (a row's net, the figure before the guild's share, the storage value); Java's `Math.round` takes a half upward, which shows -0,5 as 0 beside an `Entnahme` of 1. Half away from zero is symmetric in the sign and is what Google Sheets' `ROUND` does. Alternatives offered: half upward as `Math.round` does; half to even, whose one advantage, no drift when rounded figures are added, this rule no longer needs, and which would show the confirmed 0,5 of feathers as 0 |
| 2026-09-30 | **"Exact" means the stored `double` for now; exact sums wait in the backlog (D25)** (author choice, recommendation taken, on a domain-gate finding) → domain-model.md | A half reached only through floating-point error (`0.7 - 0.2` is `0.49999999999999994`) can round the wrong way; the 03.09.2026 snapshot holds no exact half among its 210 served figures, and only exact arithmetic in the recompute too would remove the case, not a decimal read side alone. Alternatives offered: compute the read side in `BigDecimal` from the sums' shortest decimal forms now, which fixes the subtraction but not errors the recompute's additions already carry; document without a backlog row |
| 2026-10-01 | **The machine-wide build lock in `./verify` spans the whole build, not the frontend stage only** (author choice, recommendation taken, on a measured double run) | Measured on the strand's first version, before the gate's fold hardened the lock's edges (the same scope over `focus`, `bdd`, `all` and `vuln`, and the per-worktree daemon registry): two worktrees started `./verify all` together while a fourth ran `./verify format` every minute (the commit hooks' check) and foreign lanes worked. **Whole build:** the first passed after 10 min 6 s without waiting, the second waited 10 min 6 s and passed after 20 min 21 s, and a third lane queued behind them (`flock /tmp/epc-build.lock ./verify all` on the first commit, its walk) passed after 30 min 9 s; each executed 80 test classes; peak one-minute load 5.1 on 12 cores, lowest available memory 1.6 GB; 29 `format` runs took 2 to 10 s and none waited. **Frontend stage only** (`all` ran `clean build -x :frontend:npmTest` unlocked, then `:frontend:npmTest` under the lock; not landed): the two Java stages ran side by side (7 min 19 s and 7 min 30 s), the second frontend stage waited 2 min for the first, and both passed after 9 min 30 s and 11 min 50 s with 80 test classes each; peak load 11.2, lowest available memory 1.6 GB, 11 `format` runs of 3 to 13 s; no third full build ran beside it, since memory had already reached 1.6 GB with one build at a time. Its frontend stages overlapped the other lane's Java stage for about 10 s only, so that run did not exercise the 2026-09-30 failure (a frontend stage beside a loaded machine), and the scope leaves two lanes' browser tests free to overlap, where a JVM that dies mid-run has its browser killed and reported by the other lane's `OrphanedBrowserSweep`. The whole build costs each queued build one full build of waiting. After the fold, its two walk builds started together, the first under a manual `flock` since that commit's script has none: the second waited for it, and both passed with 80 test classes. During the first run a foreign lane's `flock /tmp/epc-build.lock git commit` (the interim rule) waited about 20 min behind these builds, which a `format` outside the lock avoids. Gradle starts no daemon on a relative `org.gradle.daemon.registry.base` (`Cannot convert relative path registry.local.d/9.5.1 to an absolute file`), so `./verify` appends the absolute registry to `GRADLE_OPTS` rather than `gradle.properties` carrying it. The interim manual `flock` rule (process-learnings 2026-09-29) ends here |
