| 2026-10-02 | **The project's permission rules apply again, under a managed policy that now carries the push guardrails; the project drops its own push deny** (author decision on the project's rules under the managed settings; supersedes the push deny of the 2026-06-13 git-permissions row): the managed settings no longer set `allowManagedPermissionRulesOnly` and no longer ask on every push; their `deny` covers, in commands that begin with `git push`, a push naming `main` or `master`, a force without a lease, a `+` refspec, `--all`, `--mirror` and `--prune`, plus `git reset`, `git clean`, `git filter-branch`, `git branch -D`, `rm -rf`, `rm -f` and `shred`; their auto-mode rules refuse a push to `main` or `master`, allow an agent's push, lease-force and deletion of its own `claude/<topic>` branches, and hold back on a branch a project's `CLAUDE.md` names as protected. `.claude/settings.json` drops `git push` from its deny list in both spellings → working-with-ai-agents.md, "Permissions & autonomy" | A blanket project push deny would block the push of an agent's own branch, which the managed rules allow; the managed policy is the layer every session it reaches shares |
| 2026-10-02 | **Agents push only their own `claude/<topic>` branches; `main` stays the author's** (author decision; supersedes the never-push part of the 2026-06-13 commit-workflow and multi-agent rows): every branch an agent pushes is `claude/<topic>`; force only with `--force-with-lease` and only on its own branch; a landed branch is deleted on the remote too; `main` lands by rebase and `--ff-only` after the author's review, and only the author pushes it. The agent's additions, confirmed by the author on 2026-10-04: the session that owns the strand pushes it, never a subagent; the refspec is always named and no upstream set; `--force-if-includes` rides beside the lease; the remote copy goes only once `origin/main` holds its tip; `CLAUDE.md` names `main` and tags as protected → handbook §7, CLAUDE.md | The managed auto-mode rules allow exactly these pushes, and their classifier reads `CLAUDE.md` but not the docs it links to, so the rule sits there as one line beside handbook §7. An upstream turns `git branch -d`'s merged check into a check against the branch's own pushed copy; an editor's background fetch voids a bare lease; deleting the remote copy before `main` is pushed leaves the landed commits on one machine only |
| 2026-10-02 | **GitHub is the hub** (author decision): `main` is pushed after every landing and protected by a ruleset (no direct push, no force push, no deletion; only the author may bypass it); the author reviews a pushed `claude/<topic>` after `git fetch`, in GitLens or in GitHub's compare view; strands run in an unattended agent container as well as in this devcontainer, small ones here, and that container's devcontainer variant is a strand of its own → git-state.md, working-with-ai-agents.md "Handing the author a review" | A strand on another machine reaches the author only through `origin`; the ruleset is the server-side backstop for every client, one without the managed policy included |

| 2026-10-09 | **A request whose head the server cannot read is turned into a stand-in request that passes the filter chain** (author choices, recommendation taken each time): a handler behind Netty's codec replaces every head the decoder rejects (request line or header line, too long or malformed) with a stand-in carrying the status the server would have answered (413 for a head over the limit, 400 otherwise), so the audit log and the rate limit see it before a filter answers that status, and a blocked client gets 429; a handler ahead of the codec hands the stand-in the header lines that follow the rejected line, so Netty's length limits stay as configured and the log names the real client and browser. A head that never completes is not answered and not counted until the rescue has exceeded the header budget; then it is answered and counted without its blank line (author choice, recommendation taken, the bound that keeps a stream without a blank line from being read forever; the KB states the bound by what it guarantees, and the tests pin what the rescue charges to the budget); a half-closed input counts as closed (author choice, recommendation taken, since the server never sees a half-close: Netty reports one only with `ALLOW_HALF_CLOSURE`, which nothing here enables). The line held before a rejected header line is read again only when the decoder had already taken a header, so it is never the request line, at the accepted cost that the first header is lost when the second header line is the rejected one (author choice, recommendation taken). The rescue of the header lines stays as it is: the log names the browser for heads as browsers send them, and the unusual heads where it is lost go to the backlog (author choice, recommendation taken) | The rule is that every request the server answers is counted and logged, which an incomplete head never was. Telling the request line apart by its position needs no copy of how Netty splits a request line, which a comparison of the line's text cannot keep up with. A lost browser in an unusual head costs the operator little: counting, the status and the log's privacy hold for every head. Alternatives offered: moving Netty's length limit and answering 413 from a filter (simpler, but beyond the moved limit a request is counted under its socket address without a browser); counting an incomplete head at once under its socket address (a second counting site beside the filters); only the oversized request line in this strand (the other decoder rejections pass the same handler); enabling half-closure for every connection so a half-closed input completes the head (it would change every connection, and how the framework then serves an ordinary request after a half-close is unmeasured); telling the request line apart by splitting it as Netty does (still a text comparison a later Netty release can outgrow); never reading the held line again (a `User-Agent` right before an oversized cookie line would be lost); hardening the rescue for every unusual head in this strand |

Alternatives offered: `700`/`600` for uid 1200 alone with every file operation of the script under `sudo`, which also makes the ssh user's `test -e` read a present file as absent; the narrow mode on the work machine too, unmeasured on the Windows drive; a pre-flight that refuses a mount every account can write, which would refuse the work machine's rehearsal |

Alternative: none offered |
| 2026-10-19 | **The deploy and the rollback check the ssh user's access to the database, its directory and the rollback's backup right after reading the running container, before anything is transferred or stopped** (author answer 3a on the deploy-script plan) → build-run-deploy.md, release step 3 | Under the narrow mode a wrong group would let the run stop the container and only then fail at the copy, leaving the service down; a missing database and a missing backup are refused there too, each under its own cause. ## Recorded mutations (red evidence where a new case could not start red)

- A deliverable without a test harness cannot be seen red on its own.
- Its substitute is a temporary mutation of the code under test, recorded here so a later gate
  re-runs it instead of taking the claim on trust (handbook §4).
- A test that cannot start red, adapted or new, proves itself by its mutation in the gate dossier
  (decision 2026-10-13).
- Further recorded mutations stand as dated decision rows above (2026-09-09, 2026-09-12,
  2026-09-18).

| What the cases pin | Mutation | Cases that flip to FAIL |
|---|---|---|
| `OrphanedBrowserSweep` leaves the browser of a test run that is still alive alone | `belongsToNoRunningTest` returns `true` for every process | `leavesTheBrowserOfARunningTestRunAlone`: `Expecting no elements of: ["geckodriver [pid 6255, 0 h 0 min old]"] to match given predicate but this element did` |
| A sweep that found nothing says nothing | the empty-list early return is deleted from `failOnAnyLeftover` | `staysSilentWhenNothingWasLeftBehind`: `Expecting code not to raise a throwable but caught "java.lang.IllegalStateException: killed browser processes that outlived the run which started them: "` |
| An interrupted browser run leaves no geckodriver and no `firefox-esr` behind, and the leftover is loud (the manual run the sweep exists for, since no harness can kill its own JVM) | run `./gradlew test --rerun --tests DockerBrowserSmokeTest`, wait for `firefox-esr`, and `kill -9` the JVM that owns geckodriver (`ps -o ppid= -p $(pgrep -x geckodriver)`); `--rerun` matters, an up-to-date `:test` opens no browser at all | geckodriver reparents to pid 1 with Firefox under it and survives the run; the next run's `DockerBrowserSmokeTest > initializationError` fails naming `geckodriver [pid 11209, 0 h 0 min old]` and its eleven `firefox-esr` children, `ps` is empty afterwards, and the run after that is green |
| The link-walking step (`DashboardAccessSteps`) fails on a load-failure text either plain-German failure reads | `httpProtocolApi.urlOf` sends `page=-1` for the second page (`window.page === 1`), so the ledger's "Weiter" link opens a page that does not exist | `Every link on <page> opens its page`, example 2: `[a load-failure message on /avatars/Aurora/bank?token=test-token&page=1] Expecting ["Diese Seite gibt es nicht."] not to contain ["Die Daten konnten nicht geladen werden. Bitte später erneut versuchen.", "Diese Seite gibt es nicht."]` |
| Every price the game gave is the price the catalog holds | `STREITAXT`'s `1800` becomes `1801` | `everyPriceTheGameGaveIsThePriceTheCatalogHolds`: `Expecting empty but was: ["Streitaxt: game 1800, catalog 1801 (stock_out&selection=4&pos=1, read 2026-09-22)", "Streitaxt des Wegelagerers: game 1800, catalog 1801 (...)"]` |

| Lots are kept per item, so a deposit of one trader good never closes a withdrawal of another | every lot is keyed under `EvergoreItem.UNDEFINED` instead of its item (`openLots.computeIfAbsent(EvergoreItem.UNDEFINED, …)`) | `RoundTripDetectorTest`, run alone: `keepsItemsApart` at `RoundTripDetectorTest.java:81` (10 tests, 1 failed) |

| Without the start of the rejected line, the rescue keeps skipping to that line's end | `HeaderRescue.finishTheRejectedLine` stops skipping also for an empty start (its `startOfIt.length == 0` return is removed) | `keepsSkippingTheRejectedLineWhenItKnowsNothingOfItsStart` (`AssertionFailedError`) |
| The part of a rejected header line the server already held counts against the header budget | `HeaderRescue.finishTheRejectedLine` no longer adds `startOfIt.length` to the bytes used | `chargesTheHeldPartOfARejectedHeaderLineAgainstTheHeaderBudget` (`AssertionError`) in `onlyStandIn()` |
| A header line that fills the rescue's budget exactly is still read | `HeaderRescue.feed` stops at `used >= budget` | `carriesAHeaderLineThatFillsTheBudgetExactly` (`AssertionFailedError`) |
| An incomplete head whose rest exceeds the header budget is answered with 413 and counted once, and the connection closes | `HeaderRescue.feed` no longer completes the rescue once the budget is exceeded | `answersAndCountsOnceAnIncompleteHeadWhoseRestExceedsTheHeaderBudgetAndClosesTheConnection`: `RuntimeException` from `rawClient.answerUntilTheServerCloses(…)`, the client's read timing out |
| Blanks and tabs around a rescued header value are trimmed | `HeaderRescue.isOptionalWhitespace` returns `false` for every character | `trimsTheValueOfAHeaderLine` (`AssertionFailedError`); `trimsTabsAroundTheValueOfAHeaderLineToo` (`AssertionFailedError`); `doesNotTrimAControlCharacterAroundTheValueOfAHeaderLine` (`AssertionFailedError`); `collectsTheHeaderLinesThatFollowTheRejectedLineUntilTheBlankLine` (`AssertionFailedError`); `readsTheLineTheRejectionHappenedOnAsAHeaderLineToo` (`AssertionFailedError`); `skipsTheRestOfARejectedLineThatHasNotEndedYet` (`AssertionFailedError`); `readsTheRejectedLineAsAHeaderLineWhenItKnowsTheStartOfItAndGetsTheRest` (`AssertionFailedError`); `keepsSkippingTheRejectedLineWhenItKnowsNothingOfItsStart` (`AssertionFailedError`); `joinsAHeaderLineSplitAcrossSlices` (`AssertionFailedError`); `ignoresLinesThatAreNoHeaderLines` (`AssertionFailedError`); `ignoresSlicesOnceComplete` (`AssertionFailedError`); `completesWithWhatItReadOnceTheBudgetIsExceeded` (`AssertionFailedError`); `carriesAHeaderLineThatFillsTheBudgetExactly` (`AssertionFailedError`) |

| The slicer keeps the line before the current one | `RecentLines.moveOnToTheNextLine` forgets the line before (`previous = NO_BYTES`) | `keepsTheLineBeforeTheCurrentOneAndTheCurrentOneSoFar` (`AssertionFailedError`) |
| The slices of one line are joined | `RecentLines.keepWithinCapacity` resets the current line before it keeps each slice (`current.reset()`) | `joinsTheSlicesOfOneLine` (`AssertionFailedError`) |

## Detail questions (lower priority, to confirm later)

- **D-1 (Sheet columns 8 & 9):** exact meaning/formula of the 2nd percentage and the small integer
  count? (col 7 ≈ hunt-loot ÷ storage-deposits is confirmed; 8 & 9 are not, see [google-sheet](knowledge-base/google-sheet.md)).
- **D-2 (Other workbook tabs):** does the spreadsheet have tabs (raw protocol, item price list,
  per-month history) beyond `gid=937183112`? The CSV export only sees one. Scrape/import them?
- **D-3 (Item value source of truth):** are `EvergoreItem.marketValue`s authoritative, or were the
  sheet's values maintained separately (and possibly drifted)? How are new items / price changes
  maintained? Answered for the simple ammunition types, whose catalog value **is** the NPC trader's
  price (confirmed 2026-09-10, see [domain-model.md](knowledge-base/domain-model.md)), so at least
  those are not a guild convention. Whether that holds for the rest, and how a price change reaches
  the catalog, is still open.
- **D-4 (Hunt-loot estimate formula):** how is the sheet's estimated hunt-deposit value (col 6)
  computed? It gates the hunt-loot estimate (E3).
  - Only the display estimate is still open.
  - The valuation half is answered: crediting a hunt-loot deposit with **zero** is the guild's
    deliberate tax, decided 2026-09-10 from the guild's own announcements.
- **D-13 (Row identity on the wire):** an entry carries no id, so a ledger row is keyed by its
  position in the loaded page. That holds while a page is replaced as a whole (sorting reorders the
  same objects, and ledger paging is server-side, so each fetch replaces the array), but paging
  **inside** a loaded page needs a real identity per entry first. What identity does an entry carry
  in the contract?
- **D-14 (A thousands separator in the game's protocol):** does the game ever write a gold amount
  with a thousands separator ("1.000 Gold")? The collection reads such a line as nothing and only
  warns; if the game writes it, large deposits are lost. The scenario "A gold amount written with a
  thousands separator is not read" (`collection/unreadable_protocol_lines.feature`) pins today's
  behavior; a yes turns it into a bug.
- **D-15 (One item twice under one headline):** does the game ever list the same item in the same
  quality on two lines of one protocol entry? The collection adds such lines into one movement
  (`EntryFactory.deduplicate`), so the ledger shows one row with the summed quantity, while two
  entries of the same minute stay two rows. The scenario "Lines of the same item in the same
  quality are added together" (`collection/reading_protocol_entries.feature`) pins the adding;
  whether the case occurs at all is unknown (the `+1` variant of an item does occur).
- **D-16 (Where 1.0 is cut):** no milestone closes 1.0; one item is planned "before 1.0" (the
  failure pictures) and the skills milestone "after". Which milestone ends 1.0, and what does 1.0
  promise the guild?
- **D-11 (ToS / PII, enterprise-audit Pitfall #7):** the tool scrapes evergore.de and stores *other*
  guild members' bank/storage activity. No production pressure (Q2), but a deliberate stance is due:
  is the scraping within the site's ToS, and is storing other members' activity acceptable for the
  guild's use? Low priority; logged so it isn't silently ignored.
- **D-17 (The feature-gate reviewer's own build):** `.claude/agents/reviewer.md` tells the reviewer
  to run the full `./verify all`.
  - The gate dossier already holds a checked `./verify all` log per code tree (`walk/`).
  - Options, recommended first:
    - the reviewer reads the walk log of the tip's code tree and runs no build, so nothing builds
      in the strand worktree while the gate reads it;
    - the reviewer runs `./verify all` at the tip as well (a run of 7 to 12 minutes), as its brief
      says.
- **D-18 (Comments in the `gate/` and `deploy/` scripts):** handbook §3 lets only `hooks/*` and the
  ignore files carry prose.
  - The extensionless scripts under `gate/` and `deploy/` carry whole-line comments that
    `content-gate` does not scan (backlog G31).
  - Options, recommended first:
    - `gate/` joins the carve-out as process scaffolding of the same kind as `hooks/`, and the
      `deploy/` comments are deleted, since §3 allows none in infrastructure;
    - both are deleted and `content-gate` scans extensionless scripts by shebang outside `hooks/`;
    - both join the carve-out.
  - The recommended option widens §3, so it lands only as the author's own grant (§9: a rule is
    never widened by the strand that needs the widening).
- **D-19 (A `pre-push` hook for the push rule), deferred by the author on 2026-10-04:**
  - nothing mechanical stops a pushed `[wip]` commit;
  - on the PC nothing but the managed policy and the author's supervision stops a push of a branch
    outside `claude/<topic>`;
  - GitHub's rulesets bind only the agent machine's deploy key and read no commit message here.
  - The option on file: a `pre-push` hook in the suite that refuses, for every pusher:
    - a ref other than `refs/heads/claude/*`, `refs/heads/main` or a tag;
    - any `[wip]` subject in the pushed range;
    - any pushed commit `content-gate` refuses (a `[wip]` commit skips the hooks).
  - The hook comes with its `hooks/self-test` checks, as a build/infra strand that also goes back
    into the template.
- **D-20 (How the author merges a pull request on GitHub):** `origin/main` also moves by the pull
  requests the author merges there (Dependabot's).
  - GitHub's merge button can make a merge commit or a commit with a multi-line message, which no
    hook or gate sees.
  - Handbook §7 wants rebase and fast-forward only, one-line messages and every landed commit
    verified-good.
  - Options, recommended first:
    - merge only with "Rebase and merge" once the branch's build is green and every commit on it
      is Dependabot's (the agent machine's key can write `dependabot/` branches);
    - allow a merge commit for Dependabot alone;
    - squash with a one-line message.
- **D-21 (Case-sensitive refs on the author's PC):** the PC's checkout lies on a case-insensitive
  drive, where git's file-based refs collide when names differ only in case.
  - A fetched branch `Main` takes over `refs/remotes/origin/main`, and a tag `MAIN` stands in for
    `main`.
  - Handbook §7's case check stops every sync that sees one.
  - A background fetch between that check and a later use of the ref goes unchecked.
  - Options, recommended first:
    - move the PC checkout's refs to reftable (`git refs migrate --ref-format=reftable`), which
      keeps them out of the file system and which every git that opens the checkout, the host's
      included, must then read;
    - keep the file-based refs and the case check alone;
    - make the checkout's directory case-sensitive on the Windows host.

## Assumptions currently baked into the plan (challenge if wrong)

1. Single-guild, low-traffic tool; correctness & maintainability ≫ performance.
2. The author wants to *continue* the rebuild, not abandon it.
3. The verified Gildenmehrwert formula (deposits − withdrawals across bank+storage) is the
   intended definition of member contribution.
